The Music She Chose","volume":"婚后外传：屋顶舞会的真实独奏","anchor":"chapter-0134","paragraphs":["The teacher arrived carrying a battered briefcase and the expression of a woman willing to quarrel in formal shoes. Vesta tried to monopolize the doorway. Cassian stepped aside for the teacher, and Vesta had to move with him or stand directly in her path. The manoeuvre was so courteous that Mara nearly laughed. Her husband looked entirely innocent, a role at which he was becoming alarmingly proficient.","They gathered beside the model spacecraft. The technician showed the saved schedule and the stereo track. Lena had not missed her entrance; the wrong recording had started first. Errors could result from a mistaken file selection. Here the change history identified Vesta's account, and the photographer remembered her making the change. It was an overt action, not a mysterious symptom of a nervous player.","Vesta said she had been improving the show. A business-friendly broadcast needed consistency. The teacher replied that a live player was not a faulty accessory to a pre-recorded performance. The corporate board could judge its sponsorship after hearing what had actually happened. No electoral campaign was entitled to invent a musician's endorsement either, and one draft promotional caption had quietly linked Lena to a candidate she had never supported.","The photographer was puzzled by that caption. He had been asked to shoot an advertisement, not a concert record. To be fair, he had not seen Lena's agreement. He offered to show it now. Vesta's response became strained. The document she produced gave permission for tonight's performance; it did not turn every future image into political material. The teacher would acquaint the board with the difference.","Mara saw Vesta pocket the small recording unit, then move toward the service stair. She followed at an ordinary walking pace. At the landing, Vesta broke into a run. Her heel caught a lifted tile, and the unit slid beneath the stair rail. Mara had to dive forward to catch it by its wrist cord before it fell. Vesta tried to grab it back and struck the railing instead. Cassian reached the landing a breath later, his stage sword still hanging absurdly at his hip.","\"I must confess,\" he said, \"this costume makes effective pursuit difficult.\"","Mara was holding onto the rail. \"Hold onto this instead.\"","She passed him the recovered unit while the teacher called the event manager. They did not have to imprison anyone backstage to preserve an ordinary device. The manager asked Vesta to wait with him and recorded whose hands it had passed through. Vesta's scornful laugh no longer silenced Lena, who had followed at a safer pace with her teacher.","The corridor opened onto the delivery yard. A wagon stood beside the trench, and the broken streetlight left a patch of poor visibility. Dara moved the waiting spade behind the marked work barrier; it had fallen into the walking route. Kit would weave matching thread into a small tear later. For now, she checked that no one had damaged the repaired textile panel. At the edge of the yard, an electric gate closed only after the marshal confirmed that everyone was clear. An expensive gadget could not substitute for looking.","Upstairs, the transistor amplifier had lost a connection. This fault was mechanical and entirely unrelated to Vesta. A small meter required a fresh cell before he could verify the fault. The technician replaced the damaged fixture and checked the signal filter. A resistant screw required the correct tool. Mara refused to let anyone turn every inconvenience into proof of sabotage. The real conduct was serious enough without embellishment.","Lena sat by the bathroom door, still wearing one slipper. She had forgotten where the other was. A thermometer on the corridor wall had fallen, so Kit rehung it before looking for the shoe. She found it beside the soap dispenser and cleaned a mark from it with a damp swab. The little kindness undid her more than the argument had. She cried quietly into the wool wrap, then laughed when Cassian offered his unused handkerchief with exaggerated ceremony.","\"A wardrobe department of one,\" he said.","Mara took the wrap and settled it around Lena again. \"A very expensive department. Terrible shoes.\"","Before the concert, the teacher asked Lena to ponder whether she still wanted to play. She could decline without surrendering her instrument loan. That mattered. A stressed-out person might say yes merely to make the room stop watching. Lena asked for twenty minutes, then chose the piece herself. When she stood, her face was radiant in a quieter way. She was unafraid of the music again, if not of everything around it.","The rehearsal gave a noteworthy result: the live violin sounded excellent without a backing track. A trumpet player arrived to support the last movement. The invitation had been sent in the early days of planning, then virtually buried beneath sponsor emails. He had kept the original fax because his old machine still worked better than his phone. The timestamp helped the manager restore the intended running order.","Cassian found a small candle beside a fridge and moved it away from the paper programmes. The candle was unlit, purely decoration. A pressure gauge on a drinks dispenser needed checking by the caterer, so nobody touched it. Mara admired his careful, habitual attention more than any sword flourish he could have attempted. He noticed her watching and offered the slightest bow.","Vesta's final overture was a threat to withdraw the fee. The shrewd teacher produced the signed payment terms. The manager would deal with those after the performance, without forcing a player to negotiate alone in her costume. The atmosphere did not become perfectly easy. It became possible to hear a tuning note without flinching.","Mara and Cassian reached the roof just as the conductor raised a hand. The skyline glimmered above them. For once, they were willing to let someone else take the lead."],"questions":[],"wordCount":976,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0134\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：屋顶舞会的真实独奏</div><h1>2. A Dance After the Applause","volume":"婚后外传：屋顶舞会的真实独奏","anchor":"chapter-0135","paragraphs":["Lena's first note stopped a conversation at the back of the roof. It was not loud. It was clear enough to make the interruption feel wasteful. Mara stood beside Cassian beneath a wreath of tiny lights and forgot, for several minutes, to look for a concealed exit. The violin carried over the river. When the trumpet entered, a child near the front sat upright as though the sound had addressed him personally.","Cassian had removed his stage sword before sitting down. Mara was grateful; he had spent the last hour trying to avoid striking furniture with it. His velvet coat looked unfairly good against the night. She touched the finely worked cuff and thought, with private satisfaction, that the man inside it had chosen her every morning since their marriage. She intended to keep giving him reasons to smile about that choice.","The concert ended in a hush, then applause. Lena was radiant when she bowed. Her teacher came forward without a grand introduction and stood at her side. In the early days of the programme, a bow had meant thanking whoever could keep the lights on. Tonight it also meant trusting that the room had heard her own work. That change was noteworthy even if a balance sheet could not describe it.","Vesta had left with the manager after giving her account. The board would review the event files and the disputed promotion. Lena did not have to decide every consequence before taking off her costume. She was tired, virtually shaking with release, and still a little stressed-out. Her teacher brought her to the quiet corner instead of treating applause as proof that no harm had been done.","Mara found Kit repairing the last section of the textile border. Dara sat nearby, holding the repaired gown above the dusty floor. A candle-shaped electric lamp threw a warm glow over their hands. \"We should charge per rescue,\" Dara said.","\"We'd become unbearable,\" Kit replied.","\"We already are. Look how happily she admits it.\"","Mara laughed and helped them tidy the tools. The room had become a temporary home to so many borrowed things that returning each one mattered. A musician could inhabit a stage for an hour without the sponsor owning her future. A restorer could repair a curtain without surrendering her name. Those distinctions were easier to say after somebody had defended them in practice.","The event manager asked Lena's teacher to help improve next year's arrangements. A shrewd organiser could welcome business without handing over the programme. The new terms would be business-friendly in a useful sense: clear fees, honest promotion and the same rules for every supporter. Vesta's electoral caption would not be used. The candidate's office had not requested it and confirmed that fact. Nobody needed to invent a larger conspiracy to justify a small, clear correction.","By the downstairs bar, two guests argued about the incident as if it were a sporting match. One said Lena was lucky that a duke had been present. The other said the teacher had done the difficult part. Mara thought both had missed the young woman who spoke first. She had been unafraid enough to name the problem while everyone still wanted her to smile. Courage did not require feeling nothing.","The technician packed the repaired stereo equipment. He would no longer leave a live programme on auto-pilot merely because the screen appeared ready. Some failures result from routine damage; some result from choices. Checking both possibilities had prevented a false accusation against a transistor and a false excuse for a person. His summary was so dry that Cassian nearly spilled his drink laughing.","Outside, the streetlight was repaired. Workers had closed the trench and moved the spade away. Mara could finally look upward at the skyscraper windows without checking where her next foot would land. The river below reflected a slow procession of lights. A boat's deck held a small party that had heard the music drifting down. Someone waved a flag in thanks.","There was still a ball, and Cassian asked for his promised dance. Mara left her wool wrap with Kit. Her veil had become tangled at one corner, so he carefully freed it before taking her hand. The moment looked so solemn that she whispered, \"If you tear it, I shall invoice you.\"","\"An incentive to perfection.\"","He was an excellent dancer. She had known that for some time and remained pleased by it. They moved away from the crowd until the music became theirs without needing to exclude anybody else. The night wind touched the back of her neck; his hand was warm at her waist. For a while, no one needed an investigator or a thief. They needed two people who enjoyed being close.","Later, Lena joined them in ordinary clothing. The charity had received enough to fund several instrument loans, though there was still rent to meet. To be fair, the donors had offered not just a cheque but a practical introduction to another teacher. The publicity had helped; it was not a cure-all. They began to talk about the next performance and how to combine lessons with regular live sessions. She asked Mara how to stop sounding frightened when she argued. Mara took a moment to ponder that.","\"I don't always stop,\" she said. \"I try to know what I'm asking for. And I like having someone beside me who won't pretend I'm foolish.\"","Cassian looked at her, the smile softening before she could make a joke of it. Lena noticed and wisely turned to her teacher.","They left through the quiet side entrance. A drugstore was still open, and Cassian bought a plaster for a blister caused by his formal boots. The pharmacist asked a routine question about the drug he had mentioned; he answered without turning the conversation into a performance. Mara waited with his ridiculous sword, enjoying the overt amusement on the clerk's face.","\"A hazardous evening?\" the clerk asked.","\"Extremely,\" Mara said. \"The dancing was explosive.\"","Cassian took her hand and guided her toward their taxi, laughing at last without looking over his shoulder."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1017,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0135\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：屋顶舞会的真实独奏</div><h1>3. Mara liked her immediately. Cassian looked as though he had met a more experienced version of his wife.","\"I don't intend to excel at illness,\" Orla told them. \"I was hoping to remain tolerable company.\"","Her daughter Saffy stared at the floor. She had asked Mara to repair a marquetry jewelry box. She had not expected the restorer to side with a proposed outing. The radio faded intermittently as the wind rose. The weather was uncertain, the patient's strength limited, and the building's shuttle needed automotive work. Saffy was usually perceptive. Today, fear had overtaken every practical question before anybody could ask it properly.","Mara chose a detached tone because she could feel herself becoming angry on Orla's behalf. \"Perhaps we ask what is possible. Then Orla chooses from the possibilities.\"","The nurse nodded. An outing had to fit her present needs. Comfort was vital. That did not make her wishes useless. To empower someone was not to promise that risk would disappear; it was to give her usable choices. Hugh waited on the porch, holding a jar of sweet peas. He was handsome in an untidy, weathered way. Saffy called his late romance immoral. Orla said the remark did harm to a perfectly decent afternoon.","Mara set the box on a table beneath the skylight. A small key to its locker compartment hung from Orla’s bracelet. Its output as a conversation piece exceeded its storage capacity: everyone had a story about the tiny missing bird in its lid. Orla's painting showed that bird above a longline boat. Hugh had once worked on such boats; he recognised the hooks painted along the line. Durable objects could hold difficult histories as well as pretty ones. Revaluing the box would mean preserving both.","\"I'd like you to revalue it after the repair,\" Saffy said. \"For the insurance.\"","\"Not for a sale,\" Orla added.","Her innermost fear emerged in that brief correction. People had begun speaking about her things as though she had already left. Saffy looked hurt. Mara could mediate the conversation, but she could not heal it by choosing a sweeter word. The daughter needed time to understand what her mother had heard.","A volunteer arrived to tend the small garden. He laid a hose beside a pail and asked Orla to approve his sketch of the new flower bed. She could facilitate that project from her chair. He hoped to popularize its shared activities without requiring residents to pose for pictures. The hospice intranet held the plans, but a paper version was essential to her participation because the screen tired her. For instance, she could turn the sheet without finding a tiny screen control. The change was elementary and made a noticeable difference. She was not a second-rate adviser because she needed larger print.","Children came twice a week for an optional shared activity. Today a retired science teacher helped them assemble a molecular model with coloured beads. No radioactive material was involved; a dramatic label on an old teaching tin had confused a visitor. Another packet contained a diagram of the electoral college. Orla had taught civics once and still enjoyed a question with more than one stage. Her bedside was a workplace some mornings, a bedroom on others. Nobody was entitled to demand a performance every day.","Cassian helped the volunteer streamline the route to the garden. A loose loop in a cable could trip a chair, so he secured it to the wall with the caretaker. He kept the stairway clear while Mara checked a stuck window. A little lever would facilitate opening it, but she would leave any architectural alteration to the proper craftsperson. Being admired as a thief did not qualify her to redesign a hospice.","Saffy wanted to discontinue the outing before it began. Mara asked her to quantify the problem rather than name every frightening possibility at once. The nurse could advise on Orla's current comfort; the driver could check transport; Hugh could explain the landing. Furthermore, the plan could be a short visit to a sheltered viewpoint, with no boat journey. Orla wanted not a heroic expedition but rather a glimpse of the sea.","Outside, a power plant stood far across the bay. A warning poster beside the road told anglers to keep away from the industrial fence. Hugh pointed instead to the public shelter, safely separated from it. His longline years had taught him patience with weather. He was strictly against taking a passenger out merely to prove courage. \"In large part, good seamanship is knowing when to stay ashore,\" he said.","The old woman smiled at him. Saffy turned away.","Mara found her by a wardrobe, holding a miniskirt Orla had worn in a family photograph. \"She used to dance,\" Saffy said. \"I can't bear how little she can do now.\"","\"She has noticed,\" Mara replied gently. \"She may need you to notice what she can still do.\"","The daughter began to cry. Mara stayed. It would not lessen grief to rush it into an honorable moral lesson. Her own mother had taught her that love could become clumsy under fear. She would not give in to the temptation to make that clumsiness an enemy.","When Mara returned, Orla had put a bright stripe on her robe with a borrowed scarf. Hugh had produced a porcelain cup and asked for tea. Their unaligned chairs faced the same window. The care team would take each practical question in turn. Nothing was hopeless yet, and nothing had to be settled by pretending it was easy."],"questions":[],"wordCount":967,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0136\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：海边的二十三分钟</div><h1>1. A Smaller Voyage","volume":"婚后外传：海边的二十三分钟","anchor":"chapter-0137","paragraphs":["The next morning, rain tapped the window intermittently while Hugh cleaned the mouthpiece of his old clarinet. He intended to play for Orla, not launch a parade. She was wearing a wool vest over her nightdress and inspecting the repaired bird in her jewellery box. Mara had restored the missing outline with a reversible repair. Durable did not have to mean impossible to change; it could mean leaving room for the next careful hand.","Saffy arrived with a portfolio of photographs. Hugh had shaved with a new razor and arrived looking nervous enough to amuse her. Her innermost wish was obvious before she said it: she wanted the woman in them to come back. Mara could not make that happen. The nurse could lessen discomfort, but the illness would not simply heal because the garden was lovely. Orla's grandmotherly smile remained; her strength did not. Both truths belonged in the room.","\"I was unfair to Hugh,\" Saffy said.","\"Quite,\" Orla replied, then reached for her hand.","Mara pretended to examine a timer on the stove in the shared kitchen. Cassian joined her, setting a tub of washed flowers on the counter. He had taken a taxi because the shuttle was still awaiting an automotive part. The repair bill was not nearly as large as the first alarming estimate; it nevertheless mattered to the hospice budget. A fiscal summary could quantify that cost without measuring what the outing meant to Orla.","The forecast made a boat journey unwise. Hugh chose to discontinue that part of the idea. The nearby sheltered lookout remained possible, subject to the care team's assessment and Orla's comfort. To empower her did not mean making her win an argument against the sea. \"I won't give in to the weather entirely,\" she said. \"But I can negotiate with a window.\"","Cassian went to check the covered route. A longline crew had left gear inside a marked work area near the landing. The hooks must remain covered and the public path clear. Hugh spoke to the skipper, who moved a crate before it became a nuisance. Keep away from the working side, he told the volunteer driver. A sensible boundary could facilitate the visit rather than forbid it.","Then a gust tore the hospice's borrowed canvas banner from its frame. It flew across the porch and tangled high on the garden trellis. Orla's painted bird was on that banner. Mara saw her face change and decided the decoration was worth retrieving, though it was not worth a broken neck. She borrowed the gardener's ladder; Cassian steadied it while she climbed just high enough to free the caught corner. The wind made her skirt snap like a sail.","\"A fearsomely dramatic rescue,\" Orla said when Mara descended.","\"Strictly a small repair,\" Cassian replied, taking his wife's cold hands inside his coat.","Mara kissed his chin. \"You can revalue my services later.\"","\"My estimate is already excessive.\"","The canvas was intact. Its neighbour was a small garden tent, already folded against the rain. Saffy helped mend its edge while a volunteer discussed how to popularize the garden programme without turning residents into advertisements. For instance, people could photograph the flowers and ask separately before including a person. That was essential to making the place welcoming. Furthermore, declining a picture must not cost anyone attention or invitations.","The visiting children had arrived during the commotion. The teacher put a microscope safely aside before children gathered round. Their molecular model now represented three different arrangements, one of which had fallen apart. The teacher asked them to rebuild it rather than call the attempt useless. The old tin with the word radioactive had been replaced by a clearly labelled empty teaching box. Nobody had brought such material into the hospice. Saffy had been perceptive enough to ask before sharing a frightening photograph of the old label online.","At another table, a boy wanted to know why a lesson about the electoral college had so many arrows. Orla asked him to trace one route with a finger. She did not need to deliver the whole lesson at once. The boy, in turn, showed her a locomotive drawing with far more complicated arrows. They agreed that everybody could excel at making a diagram confusing. Orla laughed until she needed a rest.","Mara watched Saffy count the laugh as a victory, then stop counting. A durable afternoon was not a set of points. Illness could do harm to a family in large part through exhaustion, even when no one intended cruelty. An outside person could act as a calm intermediary, but rather than mediate every sentence, Mara let mother and daughter find their own rhythm. Her detached expression softened when Saffy stroked the old woman's hair.","A visitor offered a fearsomely expensive supplement, calling the hospice's approach hopeless. He claimed a mysterious molecular benefit and tried to make Saffy ashamed of refusing. The nurse asked him to leave his sales material outside. Neither the claim nor its price established a benefit. Pressuring a frightened family was immoral. Mara would side with the nurse, and so would Orla, who said she already had enough unsolicited advice to fill a warehouse.","The nurse had been perceptive enough to recognise the pressure. It was vital to stop the intrusion before fatigue could overtake Orla. The visitor departed, muttering about lucrative opportunities. Cassian shut the door before the room could inhale another cloud of his perfume. Mara was amused by the small, protective anger he saved for people he liked. It was noticeable even in silence.","They had followed the practical plan to the letter. The rain was easing. The driver brought the chair upright, Hugh fetched the blanket, and Orla decided she wanted twenty minutes by the water. Her unaligned scarf ends fluttered against her robe. Saffy walked beside her rather than ahead. That, more than any grand promise, would facilitate the next part."],"questions":[],"wordCount":984,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0137\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：海边的二十三分钟</div><h1>2. Twenty-Three Minutes","volume":"婚后外传：海边的二十三分钟","anchor":"chapter-0138","paragraphs":["The outing lasted twenty-three minutes. Orla insisted on recording the number because the three extra minutes had contained a very good joke. Hugh had sat beside her at the sheltered lookout and called the seagulls fearsomely overdressed. One wore a dark mark above its eye that resembled Cassian's old formal collar. Mara laughed before she could be dignified. Her husband said the resemblance was entirely honorable.","Beyond the public path, a longline boat was coming home. The crew worked carefully in the drizzle. Orla watched without asking to join them. To discontinue the boat journey had been sensible; it had not made the whole day hopeless. The distant sail, the smell of rain and Hugh's hand were enough for the moment she was in. Her innermost wish had become smaller than Saffy had imagined and no less vital for that.","Cassian and Mara waited a little distance away. They were not detached from the scene, but they did not need to stand inside it. Mara's hand slipped beneath his coat. He kissed her temple as the boat passed. She thought of every ending she had once mistaken for a warning against beginning. Loving him did not make her unafraid of loss. It made the present a place she wanted to inhabit fully.","Saffy had learned that comfort could facilitate a choice without dictating it. She helped Orla sit forward, then asked whether she wanted to return. Her mother said yes. No one had failed by using fewer minutes than a ticket promised. To empower a person was, for instance, to make returning home as easy as leaving. Furthermore, a quiet decision needed no audience to become real.","Back at the hospice, the nurse helped Orla settle. The visit would not heal her illness, and the care team did not describe it that way. Kindness was essential to the visit. It could lessen one afternoon's loneliness. A durable memory was not guaranteed; she might be tired and confused later. That did not make today's pleasure second-rate. Saffy could stop demanding that each moment prove itself against everything she feared.","Mara repaired a loosened hinge on the jewellery box and invited Saffy to inspect it in turn. The bird remained visible, with the new work documented. Orla asked her daughter to revalue it for the promised insurance record, then put it back where she could open it. Objects would be dealt with strictly according to her wishes while she could express them. Saffy agreed without sounding as if she had surrendered something vital.","The hospice wanted to popularize its garden work in the next newsletter. A volunteer would streamline the newsletter schedule. Its mouthpiece would be the coordinator, not a patient chosen for a moving photograph. Orla offered one sentence of her own: \"We planted things and argued about where the blue ones should go.\" Mara thought it an excellent summary. Emotionally difficult work could still contain irritation, jokes and an ugly flowerpot. It did not become less worthwhile for refusing a polished moral.","In the activity room, the children put their molecular model away. They had labelled each piece so another group could use it. The empty teaching tin that once bore the word radioactive went to a history cupboard with an explanation of its former use. The teacher would not allow a dramatic container to frighten people twice. Asking a plain question had been more useful than circulating a photograph detached from its setting.","The boy with the train drawing returned his worksheet about the electoral college. Orla had marked one confusing arrow for him, then stopped when she became tired. He thanked her as naturally as he thanked the driver who found his missing sock. Teaching was in large part a series of small exchanges. She had not needed to excel every hour to remain good company. The grandmotherly teacher could take a rest without resigning her place among them.","The supplement seller tried to contact Saffy again. This time she did not give in to his pressure. The pressure was immoral. A lucrative pitch could still be harmful, and an expensive bottle could do harm to her budget without helping her mother. She kept the message for the coordinator and asked him to discontinue the calls. Mara would side with her if she needed support. She did not need to mediate the answer Saffy had already given clearly.","Sleep began to overtake Orla as Hugh put his clarinet beside the bed and played softly, intermittently, for a few minutes. An honorable intention did not require perfect tuning. His unaligned notes made Orla smile. Mara sat in the garden with Cassian, who had removed his wet shoes and was considering whether one stripe of mud could be called fashionable. His sock had a hole at the heel.","\"A notice to your wardrobe department,\" she said.","\"I hoped you might count the hole as a design feature.\"","\"I can act as your adviser. I cannot facilitate a delusion.\"","He laughed and drew her closer. Neither looked up when the garden timer clicked. An old typewriter clicked in the office; Orla preferred its typed letters to email. A caretaker filled the watering pail and put the hose away without interrupting them. The little world continued around their private happiness.","Before leaving, Mara showed Saffy the repair record to the letter of their agreement. The work could be repeated by another restorer if needed. Elementary care could prevent a rushed choice from causing harm. The fiscal benefit was not nearly as important as the respect. A slippery path could result in a fall; one clever volunteer could not replace maintenance. That was a noticeable advantage of a modest repair over an irreversible flourish. The daughter said she understood now why Mara had refused to make the bird look brand-new. The life around it mattered too.","Orla was asleep when they said goodbye. Hugh asked them to keep away from the noisy gate, so Cassian led Mara through the side porch. She paused to inhale the clean air before entering the taxi. Their luggage waited in the taxi. On the ride home, she leaned against his shoulder and watched rain gather on the glass. The day had not cured anyone. It had been, in large part, a day in which people listened before deciding what love ought to look like.","He took her hand. She tightened her fingers around his and let the city bring them home."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1074,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0138\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：海边的二十三分钟</div><h1>3.
